La bibliothèque se documente elle-même · Chaînes de caractères

stzString

1118 méthodes propres · 756 formes avec extensions, rangées sous leur méthode

la source: base/string/stzString.ring(s'ouvre dans un nouvel onglet) · Toutes les classes · Le guide Chaînes de caractères

Sous chaque méthode, ses extensions (Q CS XT Z ZZ IB W U) : en gras et cochée, elle existe ; barrée, elle n'existe pas. Les extensions et leur sens

Exemples : 220 viennent des tests de la bibliothèque ; 586 ont été composés à partir de la signature de chaque méthode et exécutés pour cette page, sur un objet neuf, par exemple o1 = new stzString("banana split"). 312 méthodes n'en ont pas encore. Exemples exécutés le 2026-10-03 20:34 dans la bibliothèque au commit 0e72e2e2c.

Méthode
Explication, telle que la source la porte
operator
Haro's `obj[n]` syntax dispatches to operator("[]", n). For stzString we want the n-th codepoint (1-based). Negative n counts from the end: Q("ABCDE")[-1] = "E", [-2] = "D".aucune extension
init
Build the string object from the given Haro string.aucune extension
Content
Override stzObject.Stringified/ToString. The parent returns ObjectName() (which is "@noname" for unnamed objects); stzString must return its actual content. Without this override, stzHashList.Classes() returned 4x "@noname" instead of the unique value labels. 6 exemples exécutésaussi écrite: Text · AsWell · String · ToString · TheString · Stringified · DeepStringifieddans la section getting content of the string
o1 = new stzList([ 1, 2, 3 ])
? @@( o1 + Q(4) + 5 )
? @@( (o1 + Q("X" : "Z")).content() )
#--> [ 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 ]
#--> [ 1, 2, 3, [ "X", "Y", "Z" ] ]
test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWU
InfereType
Infer the canonical Softanza type named by this string. Used by the global InfereType(cType). Scalars collapse to :Number/:String/:Char (Stz-prefixed to :StzNumber/...); non-stz list/pair types collapse to the generic :List; stz-prefixed list types keep their (de-pluralised) canonical name (e.g. StzListsOfNumbers -> :StzListOfNumbers). Delegates to a file-scope helper: inside this class StartsWith / StzFind / StzReplaceCS resolve to stzString METHODS (wrong arity -> R20); at file scope they resolve to the global funcs.
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? o1.InfereType()
#--> unknown

FindNext
Same as FindNextCS: FindNext: find the next occurrence of pcSubStr starting AFTER position nStart (so FindNext("x", 0) is equivalent to FindFirst). Ported from the legacy monolithic archive (~line 50328). Uses Haro's substr() with start-position support so it works for both case-sensitive and case-insensitive search without pulling in engine-level changes. 6 exemples exécutésaussi écrite: FindNextSubString
o1 = new stzString("123456789")
# nothing at or after 10
? o1.FindNext("1", :startingAt = 10)
#--> 0

NumbersComingAfter
Same as NumbersComingAfterCS: Numbers: extract every number literal embedded in the string as a list of strings, preserving signs and decimal points. Example: "book: 12.34, watch: -56.30, glasses: 77." -> [ "12.34", "-56.30", "77" ] Ported from the legacy monolithic archive (~line 100884) -- pure char-scan, self-contained, depends only on Chars(). Used by stzTimex.ParseDurationToMinutes() and is the #3 most-called missing method in the catalog. -- NumbersComingAfter: find every signed/decimal number literal that follows an occurrence of pcSubStr in the string. Returns a list of strings (the textual number forms incl. sign). Example: new stzString("This[@i-1] = This[@i+3]").NumbersComingAfter("@i") -> [ "-1", "+3" ] Ported from archive line 101153 but kept self-contained: manual scan instead of stzRegex + WithoutSpaces dependency. Used by stzCCode and friends.aussi écrite: NumbersAfterQCSXTZZZIBWU

ToListOfStzChars
Vowels: return the list of vowel chars in the string (ASCII a/e/i/o/u, case-insensitive). Ported from archive line 103163; self-contained byte scan. NumberOfVowels / VowelN are simple count aliases; VowelsB / HasVowels are predicates. Transform the chars of the string into a list of stzChar objects (each backed by stzStringChar via the alias).
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? @@( o1.ToListOfStzChars() )
#--> [ @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname, @noname ]

ReplaceInSection
ReplaceInSection: replace occurrences of pSubStr within the section [n1, n2] with pNew. Polymorphic on argument order: ReplaceInSection(n1, n2, pSubStr, pNew) -- bounds first ReplaceInSection(pSubStr, pNew, n1, n2) -- substrings first 1 exemples exécutés
# FindZZ locates the section of Programming
? @@( Q("Softanza Programming by Heart").FindZZ("Programming") )
# ReplaceInSection m->M inside [10,20]
o = new stzString("Softanza Programming by Heart")
o.ReplaceInSection("m", "M", 10, 20)
? o.Content()
#--> [ [ 10, 20 ] ]
#--> Softanza PrograMMing by Heart

UppercaseSubString
UppercaseSubString: uppercase a section or a named substring. Accepts: - UppercaseSubString(pcSub) : every occurrence - UppercaseSubString([n1, n2]) : 1-arg list form 1 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("Thank you Irwin Rodriguez!")
o1.UppercaseSubString("Irwin")
o1.AddXT( 2Hearts(), :Around = "IRWIN" )
# Irwin is uppercased and hearted
? o1.Content()
#--> Thank you ♥♥IRWIN♥♥ Rodriguez!

Shorten
Shorten: truncate the content to the first N chars + "..." if it's longer than N. Defaults to N = 30. The "shorten the MIDDLE" family: keep N chars from each side and join them with an ellipsis. Shorten/ShortenN mutate; Shortened/ShortenedN return. 1 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
o1.Shorten()
? o1.Content()
#--> ban...lit

Boxify
Boxify: surround the content with the Softanza box -- the SAME box-drawing glyphs as tables, grids, timelines and calendars (the VISUALITY design system; never plain +/- bars). Returns the boxed string; the content-mutating form is Box()/BoxXT().
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? o1.Boxify()
#--> ┌──────────────┐
#--> │ banana split │
#--> └──────────────┘

WordsForSearch
Like Words() but CJK runs become OVERLAPPING CHARACTER BIGRAMS -- the dictionary-free CJK indexing baseline (cf. Lucene CJKBigramFilter), much better recall than per-character for CJK search/matching. Non-CJK words are unchanged. (True dictionary word segmentation would be a future ICU step.)aussi écrite: SearchTokens
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? @@( o1.WordsForSearch() )
#--> [ "banana", "split" ]

Split
Split the string into a list of parts around a separator. 6 exemples exécutésaussi nommée: split separate tokenize break apart delimiter explode cut into parts separate tokenize break apart cut into parts explode divide on
# Split on the literal hyphen
? @@( Q("ONE-TWO-THREE").Split("-") )
# SplitW at every non-letter is equivalent
? @@( Q("ONE-TWO-THREE").SplitW('{ Q(@char).IsNotLetter() }') )
#--> [ "ONE", "TWO", "THREE" ]
#--> [ "ONE", "TWO", "THREE" ]
test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWU
SplitToNParts
SplitToNParts: split the string into fixed-size character chunks of n characters each. The split is RIGHT-anchored (counting from the end of the string), which is the natural shape for digit grouping -- "1234567" with n=3 gives ["1", "234", "567"]. The Q form returns a stzList wrapper for fluent chaining.aussi écrite: SplitInNParts
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? @@( o1.SplitToNParts(2) )
#--> [ "banana", " split" ]
composé à partir de la signature, exécuté pour cette pageQCSXTZZZIBWU
Trimmed
A copy without leading and trailing whitespace; the original is unchanged. 2 exemples exécutésaussi nommée: without surrounding spaces whitespace stripped without padding cleaned
o1 = new stzString("  lots   of    whitespace  ")
# edges trimmed
? o1.Trimmed()
# then squeezed and shouted
? o1.SimplifyQ().Uppercased()
#--> lots   of    whitespace
#--> LOTS OF WHITESPACE

test de la bibliothèqueaucune extension
DiacriticsRemoved
Dotless / DotsRemoved: return the content with dots stripped from "i" and "j" (Unicode "Latin Letter Dotless I" / "Dotless J"). Lossy on purpose -- meant for the typographic effect, not for round-trippable encoding. Port from archive line 93405. DiacriticsRemoved: strip combining diacritic codepoints from the content. Uses NFD normalisation to decompose accented letters into base + combining marks, then drops any codepoint in the combining ranges: U+0300-U+036F Combining Diacritical Marks (Latin etc.) U+0610-U+061A Arabic U+064B-U+065F Arabic Tashkil (Fatha, Damma, ...) U+06D6-U+06ED Arabic supplementals U+0951-U+0954 Vedic / Devanagari 1 exemples exécutésaussi écrite: WithoutDiacritics
# a French sentence loses its accents
? StzStringQ("C'était un énoncé accentuée, à vrai-dire, extrâ!").DiacriticsRemoved()
# an Arabic greeting loses its harakat
? StzStringQ("السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُمْ").DiacriticsRemoved()
#--> C'etait un enonce accentuee, a vrai-dire, extra!
#--> السلام عليكم

ReplaceSectionsByMany

test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWU
ReplaceLeadingChars
ReplaceLeadingChars(:With = pcNew) -- collapse the leading run of a single repeated char into one instance of pcNew. Examples: "___VAR---" + :With="*" -> "*VAR---" "aaaaHELLO" + :With="A" -> "AHELLO" If there's no run (the first 2 chars differ), the string is returned unchanged. 3 exemples exécutésQCSXTZZZIBWU
ReplaceTrailingChars
Replace the run of identical chars at the end of the string with the given string (mutating). 3 exemples exécutésQCSXTZZZIBWU
ReplaceLeadingAndTrailingChars
Same as ReplaceLeadingChars: ReplaceLeadingChars(:With = pcNew) -- collapse the leading run of a single repeated char into one instance of pcNew. Examples: "___VAR---" + :With="*" -> "*VAR---" "aaaaHELLO" + :With="A" -> "AHELLO" If there's no run (the first 2 chars differ), the string is returned unchanged. 1 exemples exécutésQCSXTZZZIBWU
ReplaceLeadingChar
ReplaceLeadingChar(pcChar, :With = pcNew) -- replace the leading run only IF the leading char equals pcChar. Otherwise no-op. 3 exemples exécutésQCSXTZZZIBWU
ReplaceTrailingChar
Replace the trailing run of pcChar with the given string (mutating). 2 exemples exécutésaucune extension

RemoveLeadingChars
RemoveLeadingChars / RemoveTrailingChars / RemoveBoundingChars: strip every leading (or trailing, or both) char that matches the first (resp. last) char of the current content. Equivalent to the "trim run" interpretation in narrative tests.

o1.RemoveLeadingChars()
? o1.Content()
#--> anana split

LeadingCharsAsString
LeadingChars() / TrailingChars() -- return the leading (or trailing) RUN of identical chars as a single string. e.g. "----Haro" -> "----". Used by narrative leading-char analysis. LeadingChars() returns the leading run as a LIST of chars (e.g. ["-","-","-"]); LeadingCharsAsString() returns the run "---".aussi écrite: LeadingSubString · LeadingCharsAsSubStringQCSXTZZZIBWU

ContainsEither
ContainsEither(arg1, arg2): two-arg form covers the narrative shapes. arg2 can be the :Or = "..." named-param, or any string (treated as the second alternative); if arg1 is itself a list of substrings and arg2 is "", we fall back to ContainsOneOfThese. 1 exemples exécutés
# both words present is still TRUE (inclusive)
? Q("me you all the others").ContainsEither("me", :Or = "you")
# only one word present is TRUE
? Q("me and all the others").ContainsEither("me", :Or = "you")
#--> 1
#--> 1

FindTheseBounds
Same as FindTheseBoundsCS: Find the given opening and closing bounds: their positions in the string. 1 exemples exécutés

o.RemoveTheseBounds("[", "]")
? o.Content()
#--> [ 1, 15 ]
#-->  @$2{"a";1;[1}U ]

FindAsSections
Same as FindAsSectionsCS: The [start, end] section of every occurrence of pcSubStr. 2 exemples exécutésaussi écrite: FindAllAsSections
o1 = new stzString('   str = "  ...  "     and   str !=    "  *** " ')
? @@NL( o1.BoundedBy('"') ) + NL
? @@( o1.FindAsSections( o1.BoundedBy('"') ) )
#--> [ "  ...  ", "     and   str !=    ", "  *** " ]
#--> [ [ 11, 17 ], [ 19, 39 ], [ 41, 46 ] ]
test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWUD
FindAsSection
Same as FindAsSectionCS: Singular form: returns the first occurrence as a [start, end] section (rather than a list of sections). Useful when the narrative cares about "where IS that one substring".aussi écrite: FindFirstAsSection
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? @@( o1.FindAsSection("an") )
#--> [ 2, 3 ]
composé à partir de la signature, exécuté pour cette pageQCSXTZZZIBWUST
FindManyAsSections
Same as FindManyAsSectionsCS: Plural with explicit "Many" prefix: same semantics as FindAsSections but accepts a list of substrings, with the results from all of them flattened into a single ordered list. 1 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("...emm...eh..emm...eh")
? @@( o1.FindMany([ "emm", "eh" ]) )
? @@(o1.FindManyAsSections([ "emm", "eh" ]))
? @@( o1.Find([ "emm", "eh" ]) )
? @@(o1.FindAsSections([ "emm", "eh" ])) # Of FindZZ() for short
#--> [ 4, 10, 14, 20 ]
#--> [ [ 4, 6 ], [ 10, 11 ], [ 14, 16 ], [ 20, 21 ] ]
#--> [ 4, 10, 14, 20 ]
#--> [ [ 4, 6 ], [ 10, 11 ], [ 14, 16 ], [ 20, 21 ] ]
test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWU
MultiReplaced
Same as MultiReplacedCS: ONE-PASS dictionary replace: old[i] -> new[i], scanning the text ONCE. The FIRST matching pattern (in list order) wins at each position and the scan advances past it -- so a replacement's output is NEVER re-matched (no cascade). Contrast the classic ReplaceManyByMany which runs N sequential Replace passes (N full rebuilds + cascade). For templating, sanitization and dictionary substitution. Patterns must not contain NUL.QCSXTZZZIBWU
MultiReplace
Replace each substring of paOlds with its counterpart in paNews (pairwise, mutating).QCSXTZZZIBWU
ManyReplaced
Same as ManyReplacedCS: Immutable forms of ReplaceMany. Return the new string content without mutating This. Ported from archive line 41923.aussi écrite: TheseReplaced · SubStringsReplaced · ManySubstringsReplaced · TheseSubstringsReplaced
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? o1.ManyReplaced([ "an", "a" ], "AN")
#--> bANANAN split
composé à partir de la signature, exécuté pour cette pageQCSXTZZZIBWU
RemoveNth
Same as RemoveNthCS: Remove only the nth occurrence of pcSubStr (mutating).
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
o1.RemoveNth(2, "an")
? o1.Content()
#--> bana split
composé à partir de la signature, exécuté pour cette pageQCSXTZZZIBWU
Surround
Put pcBefore and pcAfter around the content (mutating). For a copy, use Surrounded.aussi nommée: wrap enclose put around bracket withQCSXTZZZIBWU
Surrounded
The content with pcBefore and pcAfter around it, as data.
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? o1.Surrounded("an", "an")
#--> anbanana splitan
composé à partir de la signature, exécuté pour cette pageaucune extension
Duplicates
Duplicates(): every duplicated SUBSTRING (not just chars), deduped, in (i,j) scan order -- matches the original DuplicatesCS. e.g. "RINGORIALAND" -> [ "R","I","A","N", ...multi-char dups... ]. (For a string with no repeated multi-char run, this coincides with the duplicated chars.) 3 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? @@( o1.Duplicates() )
#--> [ "a", "an", "n", "na" ]
composé à partir de la signature, exécuté pour cette pageQCSXTZZZIBWU

ToList
ToList: if the string represents a Haro list literal ("[1,2,3]"), eval it into the actual list. Otherwise returns the chars. Used by stzSmallFuncs.StzN to count list-in-string elements.

#--> [ "b", "a", "n", "a", "n", "a", " ", "s", "p", "l", "i", "t" ]

SubStrings
ENGINE-BACKED (StzEngineStringSubStrings): all substrings in (i,j) order, no dedup. Retires the O(n^2) Haro double-loop. 5 exemples exécutésaussi écrite: Substrinks · Substrongs
o1 = new stzString("RINGORIALAND")
# there are 12*13/2 = 78 substrings
? o1.NumberOfSubStrings()
# SubStrings() returns that many
? len( o1.SubStrings() )
#--> 78
#--> 78
test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWUWF

UniqueSubStrings
Same as SubStringsU: Same as SubStringsCS: ENGINE-BACKED (StzEngineStringSubStringsUniqueCS): dedup, first-seen order. cs=1 exact, cs=0 case-insensitive. Retires the O(n^3) loop.aucune extension
NumberOfUniqueSubStrings
How many DISTINCT substrings the string yields. 1 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("..ONE..TWO..ONE..")
# all substrings
? o1.NumberOfSubStrings()
# unique substrings
? o1.NumberOfUniqueSubStrings()
#--> 153
#--> 120

RemoveSubStringsBoundedByIB
RemoveSubStringsBoundedByIB([o,c]): drop every bounded region together with -- its bounds (e.g. removing the "]---[" runs from "Hello ]---[Haro!]---[" -> -- "Hello Haro!"). MUTATING; returns This.QCSXTZZZIBWU
FindSubStringsAsSectionsW
FindSubStringsAsSectionsW(pcCondition): enumerate every substring [start, end] and return the sections where the @SubString predicate is TRUE. Engine-backed (stzList.FindW over the candidates), no eval(). FindSubStringsAsSectionsW(pcCondition): the [start,end] sections of every substring matching the predicate. Engine-backed -- enumerates the candidate substrings and evaluates the @SubString predicate via the list W-DSL (stzList.FindW, predicate normalized: { } stripped, @SubString -> @item, Q().Method() lowered), with NO eval(). For engine-expressible predicates (=, or, and, comparisons); for richer ones use the WF form below. Replaces the retired raw-eval FindSubStringsAsSectionsWXT. 1 exemples exécutés
# the length-4 sections containing a heart pair
? @@( Q("..♥^^♥..^♥♥^..").FindSubStringsAsSectionsWF( func s { return Q(s).NumberOfChars() = 4 and Q(s).ContainsCS("♥♥", 1) } ) )
#--> [ [ 8, 11 ], [ 9, 12 ], [ 10, 13 ] ]
test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWUF
FindSubStringsW
The positions of the substrings satisfying the W condition. 1 exemples exécutés
# the matching substrings
? @@( Q("...ONE...TWO...ONE").FindSubStringsW('{ @SubString = "ONE" or @SubString = "TWO" }') )
# FindSubStringsWZZ gives their sections
? @@( Q("...ONE...TWO...ONE").FindSubStringsWZZ('{ @SubString = "ONE" or @SubString = "TWO" }') )
#--> [ "ONE", "TWO", "ONE" ]
#--> [ [ 4, 6 ], [ 10, 12 ], [ 16, 18 ] ]

FindSubStringsMadeOf
FindSubStringsMadeOf(pcChar): return the start positions of each MAXIMAL run of pcChar in the content. e.g. "..._...__" with pcChar="_" -> [4, 8]. "Made of" runs: the arg is a POOL of chars ("12" = chars 1 and 2), and a run is a maximal stretch whose every char belongs to the pool.aussi écrite: FindMadeOf

#--> [ 2 ]

SpacifyTheseSubStrings
SpacifyTheseSubStrings(paSubStr [, pcSep]): wrap each occurrence of every substring in paSubStr with pcSep on each side. pcSep defaults to " " when the second arg is omitted. 1 exemples exécutés

		"Mahmoud", "Bert", "Ahmed", "Mansour", "Ilir", "Gal", "Majdi" ])
# the names separate with single spaces, no edge spaces
? o1.Content()
#--> Mahmoud Bert Ahmed Mansour Ilir Gal Majdi

SplitAtCharsW

#--> [ "ing", "ing", "ing" ]
#--> [ "ing", "ing", "ing" ]

Bounds
Bounds(pcOpen, pcClose): the [open, close] positions of the first bounded match. Two-arg form. Bounds(): auto-detect leading and trailing non-letter runs - e.g. "<<Haro>>" -> [ "<<", ">>" ], "---Ring___" -> [ "---", "___" ]. Falls back to [ firstchar, lastchar ] for fully alphanumeric input. Bounds(): auto-detect the leading/trailing bound. Each side is the maximal run of the SAME edge char (when it is a bound char), so "<<Go!>>" -> [ "<<", ">>" ] -- the "!" is content, NOT swallowed into the trailing bound (the old "any non-alpha" walk greedily took "!>>").
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? @@( o1.Bounds() )
#--> [ "b", "t" ]

NumberOfConsecutiveSubStrings
Consecutive (adjacent) substrings, per the original monolith: for each length i in 1..floor(n/2), the string is tiled from each of the i phase offsets into i-char windows (phase-major order). Across all phases that yields every window of length i, so the count per length is n-i+1 and the total is Sum(n-i+1). 1 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? o1.NumberOfConsecutiveSubStrings()
#--> 57

InQ
Narrative-sub accessor used by SubStringQ(["sub", :In = "host"]). Stored as the LAST line of the wrapped content separated by char(1), so we don't need a class-level attribute that Haro would require declaring with the other @aContent slots. InQ(host): the host becomes the new content; the prior content is kept as the narrative sub.aucune extension
SubStringQ
Open a NARRATIVE chain about the given substring (ComesBefore / ComesAfter...).aucune extension
@
@(pcSub): narrative-set the subject substring; returns self.aucune extension
@RemoveItQ
@RemoveItQ(): remove every occurrence of the narrative sub.aucune extension
AndThenQ
Narrative glue: returns the object unchanged, chainable.aucune extension
AndQ
Narrative glue: returns the object unchanged, chainable.aucune extension
SpacifyItQ
Spacify the content (chars separated by spaces), chainable.aucune extension
SpacifyItR
The R-suffixed form returns the RESULT string (not the object).

SubStringsSpacified
Same as SubStringsSpacifiedCS: SubStringsSpacifiedCS / TheseSubstringsSpacifiedCS: wrap every occurrence of any substring in pacSubStr with spaces in the string content, return result. Used by stzCCode.Transpile to normalise token boundaries for downstream regex passes. Ported from archive line 91253; implementation collapses to a simple repeated substr-replace (Haro's built-in is global). Case-insensitive path uses a case-folded scan + position-aware splice so the result preserves the original casing of non-matched chars. 2 exemples exécutésaussi écrite: TheseSpacified · TheseSubstringsSpacified
o1 = new stzString("iloveringprogramminglanguage")
? o1.TheseSubStringsSpacified([ "php", "ruby" ]) # Nothing happens because these substrings
#--> iloveringprogramminglanguage
test de la bibliothèqueQCSXTZZZIBWU
SpacifySubStringsUsing
Same as SpacifySubStringsUsingCS: SpacifySubStringsUsing: in-place variant of SubStringsSpacified that lets the caller choose the separator (default would be a single space; this form makes it explicit). Wraps every occurrence of each substring in pacSubStr with the given separator on both sides. SpacifySubStringsUsingCS: find ALL the substrings' sections at once (sorted), drop the ones strictly included in their predecessor (so "in" inside "Haro" survives as part of "Haro"), bound each kept section with the separator on BOTH sides, then collapse duplicate separators (adjacent tokens share one) and strip them from the edges. Satisfies the archive triple: blocks 133, 306, 307. 2 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("whatisyournameplease?")
o1.SpacifySubStringsUsing(["is", "your", "name"], "_")
? o1.Content()
#--> what_is_your_name_please?

? o1.AverageWordsPerSentence()
#--> 2
composé à partir de la signature, exécuté pour cette pageaucune extension

Sentences
UAX#29 sentence segmentation (engine-backed), aligned with NumberOfSentences() -- correctly keeps "Dr. Smith", "3.14", "U.S.A." as one sentence rather than splitting on every '.'. Bulk native-list bridge (O(n)). 2 exemples exécutés
o1 = new stzString("banana split")
? @@( o1.Sentences() )
#--> [ "banana split" ]

UrlDecode
URL-decode the content in place (mutating).QCSXTZZZIBWU
HtmlEncoded
The string with the HTML-special chars encoded as entities. Engine-backed. This built a per-character LIST of the whole string and concatenated onto a growing result -- the wrap-to-scan shape, quadratic in the string length. The engine does the same five substitutions (& < > " and ' -> &#39;) in one pass, and the bridge now sizes its buffer to fit rather than silently returning "" past 64 KB.aucune extension
HtmlEncode
HTML-encode the content in place (mutating).aucune extension
HtmlDecoded
The engine makes ONE left-to-right pass, consuming each entity whole and advancing past it, so a decoded '&' can never re-trigger a match. It also covers &apos; &nbsp; and numeric entities (&#NN; / &#xHH;), which the five-replace version silently left intact -- a widening, not a behaviour change for anything the old code handled.aucune extension
HtmlDecode
HTML-decode the content in place (mutating).aucune extension

EscapedForRegex
The string with the regex special chars escaped. One engine pass. This used to build a per-character LIST of the whole string (@oString.Chars()) and scan the metacharacter set for each one, which allocates a Haro object per character to answer a question about 14 ASCII bytes. Same set, same result.aucune extension

StzType
Each Softanza class overrides StzType() to report its own type -- (the base stzObject returns :stzObject). Restored for stzString. 4 exemples exécutés
? Q("ABC") * 3
? ( Q("ABC") * Q(3) ).StzType() + NL
? ( Q("ABC") * Q(3) ).Lowercased()
? Q("ABC") * " -> "
? ( Q("ABC") * Q(" -> ") ).Lowercased()
#--> ABCABCABC
#--> stzstring
#--> abcabcabc
#--> A -> B -> C ->
#--> ...

Softanza

  • English

La plateforme des makers du logiciel à l'ère de l'IA. Née en Afrique. Utile au monde !

Chaque affirmation de ce site renvoie au fichier, au garde ou au rendu qui la prouve. Chaque bloc de code qui s'exécute a été exécuté le soir de la publication et sa sortie est à côté ; les autres le disent.

Les textes de ce site ont été rédigés avec un assistant d'IA, Claude, sous la direction de l'auteur ; le code, les exécutions et les chiffres viennent des dépôts. (Règle 99 de la constitution Zui : ce qui est fait par une machine le dit.)

Polices Fraunces, IBM Plex Sans et IBM Plex Mono, sous licence SIL OFL 1.1, hébergées sur ce site ; le site s'ouvre sans réseau. Site généré le 2026-10-01.
